# Implementation progress

**20 October scope update:** Nexus only. Vendoria work and private credentials remain preserved, but its runtime token/callbacks/purchasing/customer offers are disabled. The owner reports the site is OK and requested no SSH check; no server investigation was resumed. Supplier implementation is local and not deployed. Live remains the previously released schema43 application.

## PRICESYNC174 — automatic supplier price sync (22 October 2026)

A worker job checks published/paused Nexus shop products every 8 hours (configurable 1h–168h) with GET-only fresh quotes. Markup-priced products get a new immutable release at the new cost plus their published markup (same exact-decimal, single half-up rounding as checkout); explicitly overridden prices are never changed and raise a prominent alert at the top of admin Shop products with old/new supplier cost and our price, acknowledgeable or adjustable through the supplier catalog and editor. Unavailable/removed offers, changed requirements and failed reads keep the last good price and are recorded; a run-wide outage fails the window without marking products. Migration49 adds `supplier_price_runs` and `supplier_price_reviews`. Supplier purchases stay disabled. Details, qualification and failures: [supplier price sync](supplier-price-sync-2026-10-22.md); design review: [PRICESYNC174_DESIGN](redesign/reviews/PRICESYNC174_DESIGN.md).

## WANTED173 — wanted accounts (22 October 2026)

Administrators can publish plain-text “wanted accounts” per game; each game's Accounts page shows the shown entries (at most 6) after the listings with one “Sell your account” action into the unchanged sell flow, only while the game accepts account sales. Migration48 adds `wanted_accounts`; staff changes need recent verification and are audited. The independent senior designer decided the list-board presentation and passed it after three rendered review rounds. Focused Go/component/fixture-browser checks and six isolated server TiDB scenarios passed; the new readiness regression caught a startup-blocking index misclassification before commit. No full gate. Signed `746e545` is deployed and verified at schema48 after the owner's exact recovery approval; exact CI39708635082, image release39710595717, preflight, eight-service checks and seven live read-only browser cases passed. The first preflight exposed object storage refusing writes at its 20% disk floor (live uploads briefly refused); removing only the Go build cache and this task's test source clones restored writes, leaving about 21% free. Off-host recovery is still absent and rollback over48 deletes wanted-account entries. See [scope and evidence](wanted-accounts-2026-10-22.md) and [design review](redesign/reviews/WANTED173_DESIGN.md).

## Repository policy

One monorepo, independent Go/React projects, direct commits and pushes to `main` after both projects' `pre-commit` gates and root checks pass. See [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md).

## Current stage

- P00 technical compatibility: committed and pushed to `main` as `efabfe3e204cb37f39a16453d5a27abbdc26012e`. The remote commit hash was verified.
- P01 foundations: committed and pushed as `470e634245b3e0a51f9c40029928c4486f3b8c8e`. The remote hash was verified; remote GitHub Actions remains unverified because the unauthenticated API returns HTTP 404.
- P02 identity: committed and pushed as `60b2778abb2f2569a1af8a9101769c9c3583a81c`; the remote hash was verified. Staff MFA at every sign-in is recorded as decision D11.
- P03 catalog: committed and pushed as `aab69d22d1948ba7ba8185bd9d5ee42666bc0783`; the remote hash was verified.
- P04 pricing: committed and pushed as `37d7c45685b28afbfdf3b7cdf6a47f80666fea69`; the remote hash was verified.
- P04 coupons: implemented and verified; delivery is carried by the coupon stage commit and mandatory full hook. P04 policy/pricing scope is complete. Transactional order/payment usage transitions remain P05–P07.
- P05–P12: not started.

## P00 verification evidence

Verified locally on 9 September 2026:

- Go 1.27.1, Ent 0.14.6, Goose 3.28.0, Cobra 1.10.2, MySQL driver 1.10.1, TiDB 8.5.7 (image pinned by digest).
- `make -C backend pre-commit`: formatting, module tidy/verification, deterministic Ent generation, vet, the configured golangci-lint 2.13.2 suite, standalone gosec 2.29.0, govulncheck 1.8.0, unit/race tests, real TiDB tests, migration validation, and CLI build passed.
- TiDB scenarios: read-only empty status; sequential install and upgrade; preserved existing user data; Ent JSON/default/unique-query behavior; transaction rollback; conditional updates with one winner; supported down/up; duplicate invocation; held lock; concurrent runners without duplicate versions; altered SQL/checksums; SQL snapshot immutability; unknown/missing/mismatched bookkeeping; partial DDL and blocked retry; invalid targets.
- `make -C frontend pre-commit`: formatting, type generation/strict type checks, React/security/accessibility lint, two component tests, current-route coverage, full npm audit, SSR production build, and six desktop/mobile browser checks passed.
- Browser checks cover navigation, unavailable services without fake checkout/prices, 404 handling without reflecting injected markup, skip navigation, layout overflow, and axe accessibility checks. A detected mobile contrast defect was fixed before the passing run.
- `make check-repository`: repository-policy validation, redacted Gitleaks scan, and whitespace checks passed.
- A transitive `golang.org/x/mod` advisory was fixed by updating to 0.41.0; the final govulncheck report contains no known vulnerabilities.

These checks validate the implemented compatibility/tooling slice. They do not claim authentication, payments, orders, production topology, or every future application scenario is implemented or secure. TiDB unistore tests do not establish distributed production availability. Frontend runtime checks used host Node 26.0.0; P01 also verifies Node 24.21.0 LTS inside the production container. The ESLint 9/accessibility-plugin compatibility constraint is tracked in the frontend README.

## Open business inputs

Payment provider/regions/networks, seller settlement arrangement, win-rate promise, actual catalog prices, launch languages, and production infrastructure remain unresolved. Foundation work can proceed independently.

## P01 verification evidence

Verified locally on 9 September 2026 before stage delivery:

- Backend `make pre-commit`: formatting, tidy, deterministic generation, vet, broad golangci-lint, standalone gosec, govulncheck, module verification, race tests, TiDB integration, packaged SQL validation, all three binary builds, Compose validation, Docker build and Trivy image scan passed.
- API: live/ready/status routes, published JSON Schema response validation, unavailable dependencies, unsupported methods, bounded bodies, panic redaction, request IDs and security headers, no secret reflection/logging, configuration rejection and graceful process shutdown.
- Persistence: existing migration scenarios still pass with the third SQL migration. Readiness rejects missing schema/columns and a missing idempotency unique constraint. Queue tests cover SQL and Ent transaction rollback, idempotent enqueue/conflicts, exact large JSON integers, invalid payloads, concurrent claims with one winner, ownership changes, stale acknowledgement/renewal/failure rejection, backoff, exhausted leases, handler failures/panics, unsupported kinds, completion and shutdown cancellation.

Portal title omissions were detected by axe and fixed.
- `make -C backend dev-up`: local credential generation preserves existing `.env`, separate runtime/migration roles, explicit migration container, API/worker processes, Mailpit and SeaweedFS S3. ## P02 verification evidence

Verified locally on 9 September 2026, with the same full gate required again by the commit hook:

- Email/Google passwordless authentication, explicit account linking, verified-email changes, independently reviewed recovery, opaque server sessions, shared TiDB throttles, profile/session settings, role controls and audited first-owner bootstrap are implemented. Every staff sign-in requires a second factor; recovery codes replace only that factor. Customer, booster, administrator and owner capabilities are enforced by Go.
- SQL migration 4 installs the identity state. Existing migration upgrade/down/up, preservation, drift/interruption and queue tests continue to pass. `make -C backend dev-up` rebuilt the API/worker, applied migrations through the CLI container and passed smoke checks without deleting existing volumes. Missing identity keys were generated without rotating existing secrets; the frontend received only the matching local proxy key in an ignored mode-0600 file.
- Backend gates passed: deterministic Ent generation, module tidy/verification, formatting, vet, golangci-lint, standalone gosec including the browser test harness, govulncheck, all unit/race tests, real TiDB integration tests, SQL validation, three production binaries, Compose validation, Docker build and image scan.
- Identity tests cover browser/purpose binding, code expiry/replay/attempt limits, parallel code consumption, rolling resend cooldown across a minute boundary, shared rate limits across service instances, terms acceptance, token rotation, idle expiry, owned session revocation, MFA enrollment/replay/single-use recovery and concurrent recovery-code replacement, every role/capability combination, staff sign-in gating, suspension, bootstrap restrictions, last-owner preservation, email change and reviewed recovery, retained suspension/MFA, Google unlink/revocation, audit actor IDs, ciphertext tampering/AAD separation, encrypted mail references, delivered/expired payload cleanup, CSRF/Origin checks, public error responses and rejected production configurations.
- A simultaneous self-demotion test exposed TiDB snapshot-isolation behavior that could remove both remaining owners. Explicit pessimistic mode and READ COMMITTED transactions fixed it; five repeated concurrent runs passed, and the scenario is retained in the normal integration gate. A separate regression refuses sensitive mutations when the security-control row is missing. Same-tick sessions and no-op profile saves were corrected for MySQL affected-row semantics. Recovery-code replacement increments the authorization version and rotates sessions so concurrent consumption cannot restore replaced code hashes; five repeated replacement/use race tests passed.
- Google protocol tests use actual RSA-signed JWTs and JWKS on a local issuer. They reject invalid signatures, issuer, audience, nonce, expiration, subject, verified-email status and authorized party, and verify PKCE/state/redirect construction. Service tests reject implicit email merging, browser/state replay, and preserve provider-subject identity when provider email changes.
- Frontend gates passed: pinned OpenAPI generation, strict TypeScript and runtime response validation, React/security/accessibility lint, component/client tests, unchanged route coverage thresholds, full npm audit, SSR build, two Node server/security tests and 22 desktop/mobile Playwright checks. Final component suite contains 18 tests, including out-of-order session responses. Browser checks use a fresh migrated TiDB schema, real SMTP delivery into Mailpit, and the production SSR proxy. They verify signup/profile persistence, sessions/logout, customer and staff access boundaries, MFA enrollment, owner role changes and matching audit records, cross-origin rejection, nonce CSP, axe accessibility, navigation and mobile overflow. Desktop/mobile screenshots were visually reviewed.
- Frontend server tests verify production configuration refusal, approved terms rendering, matching backend terms versions, per-response CSP nonces, discarded spoofed forwarding headers, server-selected proxy authority/key and cookie forwarding. Both independent Docker images passed Trivy's configured fixable HIGH/CRITICAL gate; the frontend also passed Node 24.21.0, non-root, read-only filesystem smoke checks.
- Root repository policy, redacted Gitleaks, contract synchronization, actionlint and whitespace checks passed. No Ent migrations, browser token storage, site passwords, test HTTP authentication bypasses, production credentials or fixture traces are committed.

Dependency findings: `golang.org/x/crypto` was updated to 0.56.0 to fix two SSH module advisories discovered during P02. Govulncheck reports no affected symbols or imported packages. It still reports GO-2026-5932 for the unused, unmaintained OpenPGP package in that required module; no fixed version exists and the application does not import it. Gosec's G407 annotation is narrowly scoped to Go's `NewGCMWithRandomNonce` API, which generates/prepends its nonce and requires a nil nonce argument; encryption/AAD/tamper behavior is tested. Local non-Secure cookies are confined to development/test configuration; production uses Secure `__Host-` cookies.

Remaining deployment work: live Google OAuth consent and a real transactional SMTP provider have not been exercised because credentials were not provided. Public startup requires approved account/privacy text, matching terms versions, HTTPS/trusted gateway configuration, protected secrets and verified database TLS. Production distributed TiDB failure/load tests, complete data-retention/key-rotation procedures and authenticated GitHub Actions status remain release/deployment work. The current image scans retain the existing severity/fixability threshold. This phase is not a production launch or proof of every possible security scenario; order ownership, chat, financial and credential-access permissions arrive with their feature phases. P03 (configurable games, packages and forms) is next.

## P03 verification evidence

Verified locally on 10 September 2026. The full repository gate passed; the final visibility-guard migration also passed TiDB integration and local deployment checks. The commit hook reruns the entire gate on the final staged source before delivery.

Tests use a fresh SQL-migrated TiDB database, actual SMTP verification mail, session cookies/CSRF and mandatory staff MFA. No production authentication bypass or synthetic live offer is added.
- The browser workflow creates a second synthetic game, reference catalog, conditional form and fixed package entirely through structured controls; rejects a deliberate cycle; previews and publishes the corrected draft; validates customer selections and package eligibility; retains the old price while editing; rejects a stale release after republication; checks price history; pauses and archives the game. The tests establish implemented behavior, not exhaustive security or production certification.

## P04 coupon stage verification evidence

Verified locally on 10 September 2026. P04 pricing and policy/indicative eligibility scope is complete; claims must still be integrated with P05 contracts and P07 verified settlement before checkout opens.

- SQL migration 7 adds permanent coupon policies, immutable policy revisions and private contract-claim records. Ent provides query generation only; all schema changes run through the SQL/Cobra migration system. Runtime schema readiness checks the new columns and uniqueness constraints. A coupon code cannot be renamed/deleted/reused. Recent staff factors, optimistic revisions and an atomic audit/history transaction protect saves.
- Coupons configure fixed/percentage amounts, currency, inclusive start/exclusive end, minimum subtotal, maximum discount, global/personal limits and game/service/package/category scopes. The public check resolves the actual current offer and applies discounts before final rounding, preserving at least one minor unit payable. Same text across game/service/package keys cannot override a different scope's restriction. No caller-supplied amount/currency is accepted. Anonymous results are indicative; authenticated results check the caller's own limit. Held/redeemed uses count across all policy revisions; released uses do not.
- Checks create no claims. Claim uniqueness and counted states are tested using isolated synthetic records; these are not claimed as completed payment/reservation transitions. Resale policies can be declared, but resale application and seller-proceeds protection remain P09. No user/seller wallet or balance was introduced.

## P06 — Conversations, files, and notifications (in progress)

P05 is committed and pushed as `8b8c16cc9fd63d258310185ec6454cbeceba5e34`; the tracked hook passed both complete project gates again and the remote main SHA matched. ### P06 private conversations and notifications — verified stage

Implemented authenticated pre-sales threads, unique order conversations, customer/staff inboxes, immutable idempotent messages, internal notes, bounded latest/earlier/forward history, audience-specific unread cursors, staff assignment, open/waiting/resolved transitions, audited same-customer order links, eight-second reconnectable SSE hints, and polling fallback. Customers never receive internal messages, staff IDs, internal sequence numbers, assignment changes, or internal-note activity timestamps. Staff replies use aliases/generic support labels.

Activity notices and reference-only outbox jobs are transactional with staff replies and administrative order updates. Email uses generic fixed-origin links and excludes message bodies. Current preferences, suspension, and staff authority are checked at delivery. Assigned staff can receive customer-reply notices; unassigned threads remain in the inbox without broadcasting email. Admins can inspect bounded delivery failures and retry failed activity jobs after recent verification of both factors. SMTP remains at least once; ambiguous provider acceptance can duplicate email, while notices are deduplicated.

Evidence: root `make pre-commit` passed both project gates: generation/format/contract checks, vet, golangci-lint, standalone gosec, govulncheck, dependency verification, race tests, isolated TiDB integration, nine SQL migrations, three backend binaries, Compose validation, both Docker builds/scans, strict frontend types/security/accessibility lint, 37 unit/component/client tests, npm audit, production SSR/server checks, 30 desktop/mobile browser/accessibility journeys, and the Node LTS non-root/read-only container smoke. Additional live HTTP SSE testing confirms invisible internal notes and connection termination after suspension. Tests cover concurrent duplicate sends, incompatible retry payloads, ownership/CSRF/unknown fields/oversized messages, public versus internal history/read counters, link/assignment conflicts, rollback with audit failure, notification privacy/preferences/retries, and fresh-factor requirements for restarting failed delivery jobs. The tracked hook reruns the full gate on the staged source.

`make -C backend dev-up` applied migration 9 through the Cobra CLI and passed local API/worker/email/storage smoke checks without removing volumes. Logs: `.cache/p06-chat-pre-commit.log`, `.cache/p06-chat-dev-up.log`, and the stage commit hook log. The mobile support view was visually reviewed; both sizes passed accessibility and overflow checks. Early missing audit-ID, JSX-fragment, and header-style errors were corrected; no gates or thresholds were relaxed.

P06 remains incomplete until private uploads, scanning, secret storage, and their lifecycle/access tests are delivered. Attachments remain disabled. Booster message access awaits explicit P08 assignments/sharing; seller conversation scopes arrive with P09. Distributed-load qualification, live SMTP/OAuth, approved commercial terms, actual Mobile Legends data/prices, payment/seller decisions, production deployment, and remote CI authentication remain outstanding. Passing checks do not prove exhaustive security or production readiness.

## P08 scheduling stage — verified; delivery carried by this stage commit

The execution and game-access stage is committed and pushed as `24559082d536b31956aa5dbd5af7a5d07b75af84`, with matching remote main. SQL migration 13 adds staffing profiles, dated availability, immutable booking times and booking histories. Staff/customer/admin calendar and session screens support proposals, confirmation, rescheduling, cancellation and attendance records. Assignment changes and completion atomically cancel future sessions; the worker queues deduplicated reminders and rechecks authority at delivery. See [scheduling design](scheduling-design.md).

Targeted race-enabled TiDB and HTTP suites passed (30.284 seconds): concurrent overlap confirmation, customer capacity across different boosters, adjacent intervals, ownership, paused/booked calendar guards, retry/hash mismatch, rescheduling history, reassignment revocation, audit-failure rollback, reminder suppression after cancellation, attendance timing and stale revisions, CSRF, input bounds, week validation and response contracts. Clock-gap/overlap unit cases include non-hour transitions. The attendance fixture initially advanced beyond the session idle limit; it now explicitly models continuing session activity without changing authentication rules. Frontend tests passed with the existing 100% route-wrapper coverage threshold, and all 32 real desktop/mobile browser/accessibility journeys passed (30.9 seconds), including both calendar routes and confirmed play-together work. Go lint and standalone gosec reported no findings. Full root-gate and local migration/container evidence will be recorded before delivery.

Limits remain: manual dated availability, no invented no-show fees, no live settlement bypass, no automatic matching and no production capacity claim. Calendar-aware delivery rules, explicit shared booster chat/proofs, P07 and P09–P12 remain in scope. Payment-provider, operating-region, seller settlement, approved service/pricing/policy and deployment inputs are still pending.

Docker event metadata confirmed the cause; Compose and the local container now allocate 3 GiB, preserving the data volume. Booster work controls now stay disabled until the refreshed revision is ready, with a dedicated component regression test. All 32 desktop/mobile browser journeys then passed (32.1 seconds), including the final local-time display. The complete gate is being rerun on this corrected source; no failing check was skipped or weakened.

Final corrected-source evidence: root `make pre-commit` passed both complete project gates. This includes repository/secret/workflow/contracts, deterministic generation, vet, golangci-lint, standalone gosec, govulncheck, dependency verification, race tests, isolated TiDB integration (78.542 seconds), all 13 SQL migrations, production binaries, Compose validation, backend/scanner/frontend image builds and vulnerability scans, strict frontend types/lint, 41 unit/component tests, unchanged route coverage requirements, npm audit, 32 real desktop/mobile browser/accessibility journeys (31.0 seconds), and non-root/read-only frontend SSR container smoke. ## P07 provider selection and transport — qualification in progress

Operational reports are committed and pushed as `7dae7066040864a78aa7787ab834559f7f99bb0b`; actual remote main matched. The final tracked hook passed the complete repository/backend/frontend gates, including TiDB integration (239.971 seconds), 71 frontend tests, 17 desktop/17 mobile browser/accessibility journeys (30.7/28.8 seconds), required security/dependency checks and production container verification (`.cache/p11-reports-commit.log`). The final mobile date controls and report view were visually reviewed.

The owner requested a third-party crypto processor with minimal onboarding and asked us to research/select one. NOWPayments is the technical choice; see [provider decision](crypto-provider-decision.md) for the primary-source comparison, current fee/terms evidence and unresolved live settings. No country is inferred from the developer environment, no extra country/document form is added, and no unconditional absence of provider checks is promised.

The first P07 stage implements a bounded Go hosted-invoice/payment-status transport and HMAC-SHA512 callback-reference verifier. It is not registered as a live checkout route. Exact API decimals remain separate from JavaScript-compatible signature number serialization; callbacks carry only a reference for authoritative reconciliation. Invoice writes execute once and treat remote failures as ambiguous, requiring durable recovery before retry. Provider URL/redirect validation and generic failures prevent credential/private-response leakage. Offline HTTP and independent JavaScript signature vectors qualify transport behavior; no provider account, secret, sandbox payment or live transaction has been used. Durable finance state, checkout UI, receipt allocation, reconciliation workers, refunds and commercial resale remain subsequent work.

The first ten targeted test groups passed with race detection (3.001 seconds; `.cache/p07-nowpayments-unit5.log`). Initial local tests lacked permission to bind test HTTP servers; the approved rerun exposed a synthetic stalled-server cleanup hang, diagnosed from its stack and corrected with an explicit fixture release channel. No production timeout or check was weakened. Security lint first caught a direct EOF comparison; wrapped-error handling was corrected. Final review added concurrent reads/signals, commercial response echo checks and rejection of prototype-related/escaped duplicate JSON keys.

All 13 expanded test groups passed with race detection (1.928 seconds; `.cache/p07-nowpayments-unit6.log`), including 16 concurrent API reads and signature checks, exact commercial echoes and independent JavaScript fixtures. Golangci-lint, standalone gosec, govulncheck and module verification passed (`.cache/p07-nowpayments-security3.log`); the previously documented unused OpenPGP advisory remains outside imported packages. A final configuration bound and regression limit callback origins to 2,048 bytes. The required tracked commit hook will rerun the complete root and both project gates, including real TiDB/browser/container checks despite no database or frontend route changes. Staged review caught an accidental documentation overwrite; the previous progress history was restored exactly before appending this entry. No P07 completion or real sandbox/live payment qualification is claimed.

The package remains an unwired transport; full finance and real provider qualification are outstanding.

The owner's new request is documentation first: a much richer animated storefront, a scroll-driven low-to-high MLBB profile transformation, a dedicated admin frontend, and a complete production stack in the owner-created `production/` folder (with existing empty `deploy/`). [The new roadmap](../REMAINING_ROADMAP.md) inventories delivered foundations and remaining work, details the motion storyboard/acceptance criteria, admin app/modules, production topology/commands/recovery, R00–R11 execution order, all seven additions, business decisions and launch evidence. The specification, original phase document and root index link to it. Current admin routes and the existing first-owner/MFA procedure were verified in source. No application behavior, deployment configuration, migration or infrastructure is changed in this documentation stage.

The owner clarified that `production/` is exclusively for deployment artifacts, such as Compose, gateway/configuration, scripts and runbooks. This boundary is explicit in the roadmap, phase rules and specification: backend, storefront and admin source remain in their existing projects, and production orchestration references their release images.

The required final root and both-project pre-commit gates still run before the authorized documentation commit. Roadmap review checks coverage and cross-links; final gate and push evidence will be reported at delivery. Production topology and visual defaults are proposed implementation choices, not claims of deployed or completed features.

Roadmap local links/anchors, R00–R11 sequence, seven-feature table and repository checks passed. The first full hook passed backend checks (TiDB 350.709 seconds), 71 frontend tests and 17 desktop browser tests (39.4 seconds), but correctly blocked the commit when one mobile quote-acceptance assertion timed out after five seconds; 16 mobile tests passed. Trace inspection showed the accept POST still pending and the UI in its disabled submitting state, without a returned rejection. An unchanged rerun using `cd frontend && npm run test:e2e` passed all 17 desktop and 17 mobile journeys (32.2/33.1 seconds) against fresh isolated schemas. No assertion, timeout, worker setting or application code was changed. This does not establish the cause of the intermittent delay; retain it as performance/reliability evidence for launch qualification. Logs: `.cache/remaining-roadmap-commit.log` and `.cache/remaining-roadmap-browser-rerun.log`. A second full tracked-hook run is required before delivery, recorded in `.cache/remaining-roadmap-commit2.log`.

## R01 — Animated storefront and rank journey

The revised roadmap was committed and pushed as `4e65aaf38d41421fe1bbc0bde7b74fd8286d8e05`; actual remote main matched. Its second full hook passed TiDB integration (486.575 seconds), 71 frontend tests, 17 desktop/17 mobile journeys (45.1/38.7 seconds), all required security/dependency/build/image checks and frontend container smoke (`.cache/remaining-roadmap-commit2.log`).

R01 replaces the small homepage with original cinematic artwork, an emerald/gold visual system, custom/package pathway cards, ordering steps, account experience, FAQs and real navigation. Native scroll drives the same illustrative MLBB profile from Warrior to Mythical Glory, including reverse movement, an aligned emblem/frame blend, sequential rank labels, a bounded highlight and sparse decorative sparks. Section reveals retain full text contrast. System/Reduced settings, blocked-storage fallback, reduced motion, short landscape viewports, unavailable animation APIs, no-JavaScript and missing-image behavior are handled. Application security, backend APIs and root production deployment assets are unchanged; staff access now has a footer entry, while the dedicated admin app remains R02.

The first browser pass caught reduced text contrast during opacity reveals; text was changed to translate at full opacity, not exempted from axe. A later static-mode test mistakenly expected an animated transform matrix; it was corrected to assert `none`. Visual review also removed profile/label ghosting at the midpoint by aligning both frames and sequencing rank labels. No thresholds were lowered. All 77 unit/component tests and 100% route-wrapper coverage passed, with strict type checks and lint (`.cache/r01-unit-final.log`). Final focused desktop/mobile tests passed 22/22 in 7.4 seconds (`.cache/r01-browser-final.log`), including public route/axe checks and the new scene, preferences, keyboard, navigation, failure fallback, performance and CSP checks.

Final focused local SSR evidence: 1280×720 desktop / 412×839 mobile, unthrottled Chromium, zero measured CLS, zero CSP violations or remote resources, 736,427/468,372 fetched resource-body bytes, and one appropriately sized hero asset each. Recorded LCP was 136/76 ms and sampled scroll frames approximately 16.6–16.8 ms; these are short localhost measurements, not field metrics, low-end device qualification or proof of absence of jank. Build budgets enforce 12 KiB homepage JS gzip, 8 KiB homepage CSS gzip, 4 KiB shared CSS gzip, 400,000-byte desktop/120,000-byte mobile artwork and a 1 MiB browser resource budget. Actual optimized hero sizes are 368,431/100,376 bytes. Measurements and reviewed desktop/mobile hero, five-state rank and static-page screenshots are in ignored `frontend/.cache/`.

See [visual implementation](visual-design.md) for asset provenance and reproducible commands (`make -C frontend test-home`, `budget-home`, and full `make pre-commit`). Final required root/backend/frontend hook evidence will be recorded in `.cache/r01-commit.log` before the authorized main commit/push. Remaining qualifications: real field/slow-device performance, final approved game artwork/content/prices, homepage CMS/featured data feeds, R02/R03 full application redesign, all outstanding commerce flows and R04/R10/R11 production operations/launch.

The first full R01 hook failed closed at the unchanged ten-minute TiDB suite timeout (600.727 seconds, `.cache/r01-commit.log`). The active test had only run for two seconds and its stack was applying SQL migration 4 during fixture setup; this was a whole-suite timeout, not a reported quote assertion failure. The long-lived development TiDB measured about 195% CPU and 2.5 GiB/3 GiB memory. A fresh, disposable localhost-only test instance uses the same pinned TiDB 8.5.7 digest, `unistore` engine and 2-CPU/3-GiB limits on port 14001, with no application database or persistent volume mounted. Existing development services/data are preserved. The required complete hook is rerun with `TEST_DATABASE_DSN='root@tcp(127.0.0.1:14001)/mysql'`; tests still create isolated random schemas, use explicit SQL migrations, and retain all assertions/timeouts. Final evidence: `.cache/r01-commit2.log`. The test-only container `boosterplus-r01-test-tidb` is removed after qualification. This isolates the dependency; it does not establish a production database performance result or hide the original timeout.

The second attempt correctly rejected port 14001 because the test helper allowed only the original local/CI endpoints (`.cache/r01-commit2.log`); it did not run database assertions. The helper now explicitly allows the newly created disposable localhost:14001 endpoint, with a regression test covering all three permitted addresses and rejecting production names, normal database ports, other loopback ports, hostname suffix tricks and unregistered IPv6/localhost aliases. It still creates/drops only random test-prefixed schemas; no assertion, timeout or production safeguard is bypassed. This is the only backend change in R01 and affects test infrastructure, not application code. Reproduction is documented in `visual-design.md`. Final full hook attempt: `.cache/r01-commit3.log`.

## R02 — Dedicated admin application and staff workspace

R01 was committed and pushed as `668ce2c69d1cbff75b8deb1fd16f4d5a5cb81d93`; remote main matched. Its final full hook passed TiDB integration (224.922 seconds), 77 frontend unit/component tests, 21 desktop/21 mobile browser journeys (44.4/35.9 seconds), required security/dependency/build/image checks and container smoke (`.cache/r01-commit3.log`).

R02 separates the frontend into storefront and admin React Router graphs and roots, with independent SSR/client builds and separate production Docker targets/images. The customer build no longer imports the administration dashboard, user directory, owner audit or recovery screens. Shared role-aware order/chat components remain shared code; authority is enforced by the identity boundary and backend, not by hiding JavaScript. All 14 existing admin route patterns remain, and standalone servers return 404 outside their graph. `/admin/assets/*` is distinct from storefront `/assets/*`; `/admin.data` stays with the admin root. Account/security/storefront transitions use document navigation and retain same-origin cookies.

The staff shell includes grouped active navigation, breadcrumbs, order search, a native modal mobile menu, verified staff identity, sign-in/onboarding guidance, real operational cards, a dense order queue, clearer request/quote/timeline cards, and a conversation switcher with distinct customer replies/internal notes. Administration, catalog, coupons, submissions, review moderation, calendars, reports and background recovery remain functional. Root `production/` and its owner-created `deploy/` directory are unchanged and remain deployment-only for R04. No backend APIs, SQL migrations, live prices, payment claims or provider credentials were changed.

The default frontend `make dev` starts both apps with shared-origin development routing, checks both ports before spawning, refuses occupied ports without terminating their owners, and stops both children on interruption or child failure. Targeted dev/build/start commands are documented in [staff workspace operations](admin-workspace.md). Each runtime image excludes the other's build directory and has its own SSR healthcheck; the frontend gate scans and smoke-tests both as non-root/read-only containers. Versioned assets receive explicit immutable caching while private pages retain no-store. The new server/launcher suite exercises trusted proxy headers, startup policy, every admin deep link, route/asset separation, 404s, the root data endpoint, occupied ports and shutdown.

Focused qualification passed strict type checks/lint, all 80 frontend unit/component tests (100% existing route-wrapper coverage, not whole-application coverage), seven SSR/proxy/launcher tests, and final real-service browser runs (`.cache/r02-unit.log`, `.cache/r02-server-final.log`, `.cache/r02-browser3.log`). Desktop/mobile each completed 21 journeys in 26.9/31.6 seconds. Tests retain passwordless/MFA, owner vs administrator vs booster boundaries, session/CSRF checks, mobile Escape/navigation, cross-app account return, order search, catalog publication, immutable quote changes, private/internal conversations, reconnection, proof/access, intake and reports. Homepage behavior, accessibility and asset budgets also remain in the gate. Synthetic desktop/mobile dashboard, queue, order and chat screenshots are saved in ignored `frontend/test-results/` and `frontend/.cache/` for visual review.

Findings resolved during this stage: the static server's global no-store header overrode immutable asset caching; launcher initially used the wrong pinned CLI entry filename; existing catalog/report tests assumed all admin navigation lived in the customer account page and were updated to enter the staff app/use its drawer; the new conversation sidebar required a unique landmark label alongside the module sidebar. An early shell unit fixture also used an invalid short CSRF token and was corrected to satisfy the unchanged schema. Server tests initially required sandbox permission to bind local ports and were rerun with local-listener access. No check was disabled and no threshold, authorization rule or test timeout was weakened.

The mandatory tracked pre-commit hook runs the full root/backend/frontend gate before this stage can be committed and pushed; final output is retained in `.cache/r02-commit.log`. It uses the same pinned disposable TiDB on the exact allowed localhost:14001 endpoint, plus existing local test mail/storage/scanner services. The isolated test container is removed after verification; existing development data/listeners are preserved. The commit/push is withheld on any failing gate. Remaining work is R03 customer/booster redesign, R04 production orchestration, R05–R11 commercial features and launch qualification; this UI/build extraction is not a claim of public production readiness or exhaustive security proof.

The first full R02 hook stopped before tests because `vuln.go.dev/index/modules.json.gz` returned HTTP 403 (`.cache/r02-commit.log`); the canonical uncompressed index and full ZIP also returned 403. The [Go project's official storage endpoint](https://go.googlesource.com/vuln.git/+/99f357ce10e13815b479d7479aebfeb16d6c008c/README.md) remained live, with index modification metadata `2026-09-09T17:56:34Z` and an HTTP Last-Modified of 9 September 2026. Govulncheck supports explicit database URLs through its [documented `-db` flag](https://go.dev/doc/security/vuln/database). The backend Makefile now accepts only the canonical HTTPS endpoint or that official live storage endpoint, keeping the canonical default. This is an explicit endpoint selection, not automatic fallback, a cached database, a custom advisory set or a skipped check. The full gate is rerun with `GOVULNCHECK_DATABASE=https://storage.googleapis.com/go-vulndb`; all scanner flags, checksum validation, dependency versions and failure behavior remain intact. An unsupported endpoint is rejected before scanning. Final hook output: `.cache/r02-commit2.log`.

The second full hook (`.cache/r02-commit2.log`) passed the complete backend gate, including TiDB integration (214.925 seconds), production binaries, Compose validation and backend/scanner image builds and scans, plus frontend contracts/formatting/types/lint, 80 unit/component tests, npm audit and both application builds. It then correctly failed two SSR routing tests: `storefront`/`both` builds returned 503 for `/games` after about 8.3 seconds. The `/games` loader reads the catalog from `API_ORIGIN` (default `127.0.0.1:8080`) with an 8-second timeout, so those tests silently depended on a running and responsive development API; the earlier focused pass (`.cache/r02-server-final.log`) had one available. The tests now start a local stub API that serves an empty catalog listing and pass `API_ORIGIN` to the child server, and additionally assert that the storefront renders the empty-catalog state without staff markup. This removes an external dependency from a unit test; the expected statuses, cache headers, asset namespaces and 404 boundaries are unchanged. All seven server/launcher tests pass with no development backend running (`.cache/r02-server-hermetic.log`).

Work was interrupted after that run. Docker events show the development `api`, `worker`, `tidb` and `mailpit` containers and the `boosterplus_tidb-data` volume were stopped and destroyed at 19:03 local time, before this session resumed; the storage and scanner profile services and their volumes were untouched, and no committed source was affected. `make -C backend dev-up` recreated the stack from the pinned images and existing `.env`, applied SQL migrations 1–20 through the explicit Cobra migration container onto the fresh volume, and passed API/worker/email/private-storage/fresh-scanner smoke (`.cache/r02-dev-up.log`). Local development data was development-only and is not required by any test, which create their own isolated schemas. `vuln.go.dev` returned HTTP 200 again, so the canonical govulncheck endpoint is used without an override. Final full hook attempt: `.cache/r02-commit3.log`.

On 12 September, resumed verification confirmed the third hook had failed because the pinned Playwright Chromium executable was absent, and the fourth hook was terminated during TiDB integration (`.cache/r02-commit3.log`, `.cache/r02-commit4.log`). Neither produced a commit. Docker Desktop was stopped on resumption; the project test mail/storage/scanner and existing disposable R02 database were restored without changing unrelated resources. `npx playwright install chromium` verified the pinned browser installation. All seven isolated SSR/launcher tests passed again without a development API (`.cache/r02-server-resume.log`). Review also caught that the shared image-scan helper needed an explicit admin-image mapping; it now scans that image with the unchanged severity/fixability policy and writes a separate report so neither frontend result overwrites the other. The complete hook rerun is `.cache/r02-commit5.log`; all gates remain mandatory.

The fifth full hook passed backend integration (195.779 seconds), all backend checks/builds/scans, 80 frontend unit/component tests, seven server/launcher tests, all 21 desktop/21 mobile browser journeys, and both frontend image builds. Its final image scan then failed closed on two newly reported fixable Debian PCRE2 advisories: CVE-2026-86145 and CVE-2026-89161 (`libpcre2-8-0` 10.42-1; fixed in 10.42-1+deb12u1). The shared frontend Docker base stage now installs that exact patched Debian package for both storefront and admin runtimes. No severity or fixability policy changed. Full hook retry after this fix: `.cache/r02-commit6.log`.

R02 delivery completed on 12 September at `7afc56f90f97b6a7d8176b63e18eef45b8810679`, with the sixth hook passing all repository/backend/frontend checks, TiDB integration (201.139 seconds), 80 unit/component tests, seven server/launcher tests, 21 desktop and 21 mobile journeys, both frontend scans and container smoke. The pushed commit was verified against `origin/main`, and its disposable database was removed.

## R03 — Storefront, customer and booster experience

Implemented on 12 September 2026; final delivery is gated by the mandatory tracked hook and verified main push. [Experience guide](customer-experience.md) documents routes, behavior, verification commands and remaining product limits.

R03 adds scoped catalog/workspace route layouts, game/service/package presentation, a live administrator-configured selection summary, explicit continuation to order information, customer order counts/next steps/timeline, recent-conversation navigation and distinct customer messages, actual reported fulfillment progress, booster work/schedule shortcuts, and cohesive profile/security, seller, review, alert and support surfaces. Responsive columns collapse on small screens. The review histogram uses actual published counts. Named landmarks, visible focus, conditional input errors, explanatory empty/error states and refresh controls preserve accessibility. The existing staff application and every backend contract/capability/MFA/payment boundary remain intact; `production/` is unchanged.

Order submission opens passwordless sign-in in a separate tab and refreshes the session on return, preserving only the original tab's in-memory selections. Sequence guards reject stale session responses; failures conceal submission controls and renewed identity resets acceptance. Request idempotency is retained. The summary excludes hidden fields, later-stage/private free text and attachments, handles false/zero accurately, and never replaces authoritative validation. Unpaid order agreements never advance to the paid step; terminal requests have distinct guidance. Page counts identify their pagination/filter scope. No provider success, launch price, guarantee or resale agreement is invented.

Focused checks passed 102 unit/component tests (`.cache/r03-unit2.log`; 100% route-wrapper coverage, not whole-application coverage), strict types/lint, both builds and seven SSR/proxy/launcher checks (`.cache/r03-server.log`). The first real-service browser run identified missing page landmarks around the new banner/preferences (`.cache/r03-browser.log`); these were corrected, not excluded. The second run passed all 21 desktop and 21 mobile journeys in 25.6/25.8 seconds (`.cache/r03-browser2.log`). Final visual refinements expand the desktop estimator, align input/link colors and distinguish customer message bubbles; the repeated suite passed all 21 desktop and 21 mobile journeys in 30.8/32.8 seconds (`.cache/r03-browser3.log`). Tests cover real email sign-in in another tab with selections retained, unpaid quote/package acceptance, revisions/reorder/cancel, conditional fields and stale releases, favorites/alerts, private seller review, published reviews, support reconnection, hidden internal notes, proof/access, both fulfillment modes, MFA/role boundaries, CSRF and responsive axe/overflow checks. Existing homepage scroll, reduced-motion, no-JS/artwork failure and transfer/CLS checks remain unchanged.

New motion is one bounded decorative 650 ms route animation; controls/text stay visible. The same System/Reduced preference works across homepage/catalog/account navigation and honors OS reduction. Unit checks exercise cancellation and rejected animation APIs. Enforced incremental compressed asset sizes are CSS 3,995/6,144 bytes, motion surface 608/2,048, workspace 870/2,048 and browse layout 182/1,024 (`.cache/r03-build3.log`). Existing homepage budgets continue to pass. Synthetic screenshots in ignored `frontend/.cache/` include estimator, order, list, sign-in, conversations, pricing and fulfillment views on desktop/mobile; these are visually inspected alongside the browser accessibility checks.

The final hook log is `.cache/r03-commit.log`. It runs root repository/workflow/secret checks and both complete project gates, including backend formatting/vet/golangci-lint, standalone gosec, govulncheck, module/generation/migration verification, race/TiDB tests, builds, configuration/image scans, frontend contracts/format/types/lint/unit/audit/build/server/browser/axe, and both application container smoke checks. It uses an explicitly isolated digest-pinned TiDB at allowed localhost:14001 (`boosterplus-r03-test-tidb`, no persistent volume), plus existing local test mail/storage/scanner services. Failing checks prevent the commit; no hooks, tests, thresholds or scans are bypassed. The owned disposable database is removed after successful delivery.

Remaining qualification: actual device/network field performance and operator-approved live content; generic pre-order attachment inputs remain unavailable while dedicated private upload flows work. R04 production deployment scaffolding is next. R05–R11 still contain live checkout/receipts, policy-dependent fulfillment exceptions, account resale commerce, further administration/content features and public-launch operations/qualification. R03 is the redesign of implemented journeys, not a claim that the whole product is production-ready or that every possible security scenario has been proven.

The first full R03 hook stopped at TiDB integration after 206.682 seconds (`.cache/r03-commit.log`): `TestAvailabilityBoundedFanoutReconciliationAndSafeReferences` assumed one 50-subscription sweep processed all 55 subscriptions. Random UUID ordering occasionally placed the buyer on the second page, leaving the stored suppression state correctly pending. The test now deterministically selects the final subscriber, asserts the 50-delivery first-page boundary, checks queued email is refused before reconciliation, processes the remaining page, and requires `identity_changed` suppression plus exactly 54 eligible deliveries. Production availability logic and all thresholds remain unchanged. Five race-enabled runs of the corrected scenario passed in 16.577 seconds (`TEST_DATABASE_DSN='root@tcp(127.0.0.1:14001)/mysql' python3 scripts/integration-tests.py -run '^TestAvailabilityBoundedFanoutReconciliationAndSafeReferences$' -count=5`, `.cache/r03-availability.log`). The mandatory full hook is repeated in `.cache/r03-commit2.log`; no commit is made from the failed run.

R03 delivery completed as `c559d79c93ead20d4225171ec0fc434e3265d681`, with remote main matching. The second complete hook passed TiDB integration (235.574 seconds), 102 unit/component tests, seven server/launcher tests, 21 desktop/21 mobile browser journeys (30.5/29.7 seconds), all backend/security/container checks and frontend image scans/smoke. Its disposable database was removed.

## R04 — Deployment foundation

Implemented under the deployment-only `production/` directory; final delivery requires the complete tracked hook and verified main push. See [deployment operations](../production/README.md). Storefront/admin application source remains in `frontend/`; backend binaries and explicit SQL stay in `backend/`. R10 release/backup/restore commands deliberately reject execution until that separately approved stage is implemented. No public host, merchant account or live credentials were provisioned.

The immutable-image Compose stack separates the public gateway, private frontend/API networks and outbound data dependencies. Only gateway ports 80/443 are published. The gateway runs without root/file capabilities, routes admin deep links/data/assets independently, strips forged internal/forwarded headers, and preserves the exact public host. Private raw configuration files are permission-checked without shell interpolation; frontend, runtime DML and migration DDL credentials remain separate. Managed TiDB/S3/SMTP require verified TLS; scanner access is a private Unix volume with fresh definitions. Gateway IP allocation uses a reserved half-subnet outside the automatic pool. The explicit Cobra dependency diagnostic checks schema readiness, harmless scanning and a random private object round trip with bounded cleanup even after ambiguous writes.

The local qualification stack creates a fresh test CA, verified TLS data services, separate database identities and disposable application resources with `APP_ENV=staging`. Explicit SQL bootstrap/migration precedes API/worker startup. Synthetic account terms are isolated fixtures. Only the public malware-definition cache is retained between drills; application data, certificates and secrets are removed. Required checks fail rather than skipping unavailable services. Production configuration tests reject mutable images, public/overlapping IP allocation, invalid hosts/contact data, relative/unprotected secret paths, environment mismatch, exposed API ports, host networking, privileged application containers and Docker-socket mounts.

Qualification caught and corrected upstream scanner defaults: duplicate socket paths prevented the intended mount from receiving the socket, and its localhost healthcheck chose IPv6 while production deliberately binds its TCP listener to IPv4 loopback. The patched image normalizes the socket, seeds volume ownership and makes the internal healthcheck address explicit. Gateway image file capabilities conflicted with cap-drop ALL; the image now removes the unneeded bind capability and initializes certificate volume ownership for UID 1000. Automatic frontend IP allocation initially collided with the gateway static IP; the reserved pool fixes that without broadening proxy trust. Failed attempts are retained privately in `.cache/r04-staging*.log` and `production/.cache/`.

Focused backend diagnostic tests and broad golangci-lint passed, as did three deployment policy test groups and repository checks. The full required hook log is `.cache/r04-commit.log`; it includes both project gates plus the new production policy/image-scan/isolated-stack gate. Tests use dedicated localhost:14001 TiDB (`boosterplus-r04-test-tidb`) and the existing local test mail/storage/scanner resources; no production database is used. Final staging and hook results will be recorded before delivery is claimed. External provider qualification, actual domain/Google/mail delivery, clustered database/load, monitoring, backups/restores and release automation remain their explicitly assigned later gates. No check proves absence of all vulnerabilities.

The focused ninth staging drill passed explicit SQL migrations, TLS dependency checks, HTTPS redirects, storefront/admin routes, forged-header rejection, private caching/CSP/HSTS, real STARTTLS email login with Secure cookies, customer/staff and CSRF boundaries, scanner outage/recovery and database readiness recovery (`.cache/r04-staging9.log`). The final gate additionally checks storage outage/recovery and zero-exit API/worker shutdown. The worker now has a read-only schema readiness command instead of inheriting the server HTTP probe. CLI tests and lint passed after explicitly enabling the checksum database and granting Go-cache access; neither the initial sandbox cache denial nor the host's `GOSUMDB=off` environment was treated as a passing check.

The pre-gate gateway scan failed closed on fixable OS and embedded Go advisories (`.cache/r04-gateway-security.log`), including OpenSSL, curl/c-ares, x/crypto, x/net, x/text, gRPC and the Go standard library. The deployment image now rebuilds the same Caddy 2.11.4 release with official xcaddy 0.4.7, pinned Go 1.27.1, x/crypto 0.55.0, x/net 0.56.0, x/text 0.39.0 and gRPC 1.83.2. It removes unused curl and pins OpenSSL 3.5.8-r0; all source downloads retain checksum-database verification. The runtime scan remains unchanged and must pass before the stage commit. This gateway build is deployment tooling, not a third application project.

R04 delivery completed as `8af4078a592c2c2b9e203fb53460b759552731d7`, with remote main matching. The complete hook passed all gates, including TiDB integration (232.809 seconds), 102 frontend tests, seven server/launcher tests, 21 desktop/21 mobile browser journeys (25.5/29.0 seconds), every required security/image check, TLS email sign-in, storage/scanner/database outage recovery and zero-exit API/worker shutdown (`.cache/r04-commit.log`). The test-only localhost:14001 TiDB remains available for the continuing R05–R09 gates; staging application resources were removed.

## R05 — Crypto checkout and financial evidence (in progress)

The owner confirmed NOWPayments merchant registration is not yet available because the site/domain and business email are pending. Implementation and isolated provider-fixture testing continue. Actual provider sandbox qualification remains pending merchant access and is not represented as a passing local fixture test. Live checkout remains disabled without explicit server configuration and an approved published payment policy. R10/R11 remain outside the current authorized implementation scope.

R05 work-in-progress evidence: the initial race-enabled TiDB payment suite passed (24.581 seconds, `.cache/r05-payment-tests.log`), including attempt concurrency, single invoice invocation, ownership, forged/duplicate/missed callbacks, exact allocations, partial/extra/wrong-route funds, late funds, coupon/capacity release, reversals and stale observation fencing. Broad golangci-lint reported zero issues and standalone gosec passed (`.cache/r05-lint2.log`). Customer checkout/receipt and staff finance/policy screens and API contracts are being connected; their browser/component qualification and the complete stage gate remain outstanding. No R05 commit or completion is claimed.

The owner subsequently placed merchant credentials in the ignored, mode-0600 backend `.env`; only key presence was inspected. The four credential fields in tracked `.env.example` are empty. Credential environment is unconfirmed, so no authenticated provider request has been made. The official sandbox sign-in and registration pages were inspected without submitting data: `https://account-sandbox.nowpayments.io/sign-in` and `https://account-sandbox.nowpayments.io/create-account`. The owner is obtaining separate sandbox credentials. Do not infer a sandbox key from a live dashboard or activate checkout while mode is unconfirmed.

The owner confirmed replacement with sandbox credentials. Private runtime configuration explicitly selects sandbox and disables checkout; the tracked example contains no credentials or selected merchant mode. A stable local merchant namespace is configured without printing its value. Sandbox status returned HTTP 200, but authenticated `/auth` and `/estimate` reads returned HTTP 403 on 12 September. No invoice, refund or payout was created; the cause remains unknown. The dashboard callback is intentionally empty until a public HTTPS endpoint exists. Read-only maintenance can run locally, while both the adapter and store forbid invoice creation with checkout disabled. See [payment operations](payments.md).

The expanded race-enabled TiDB payment suite passed in 51.965 seconds (`.cache/r05-payment-tests3.log`). It adds strict HTTP/CSRF/ownership/policy checks, unavailable callback storage, cancellation racing with checkout and confirmations completing after timely funds detection. The first two browser attempts failed on test navigation/poll synchronization; the customer fixture now waits for completed sign-in and the checkout assertion allows the documented ten-second poll. No provider state or authorization assertion was removed. The third run passed 22 desktop journeys in 37.5 seconds and 22 mobile journeys in 35.4 seconds (`.cache/r05-browser3.log`), including ordinary staff policy publication, explicit customer acceptance, forged/replayed callbacks, authoritative fixture reconciliation, receipts and axe/overflow checks. These use a compiled test-only provider, not real merchant qualification.

The R05 implementation delivery requires the complete tracked hook, logged in `.cache/r05-commit.log`, and a verified main push. The gate includes standalone gosec and all existing repository/backend/frontend/deployment checks. Actual sandbox qualification remains an outstanding R05 acceptance requirement; R06–R09 implementation can proceed independently. R10/R11 remain deferred for owner review.

The first full R05 hook stopped at a staticcheck simplification in the new read-only origin validation (`.cache/r05-commit.log`). The equivalent condition is rewritten without changing accepted origins or disabling the rule. The complete hook is repeated in `.cache/r05-commit2.log`.

## R06 — Cases and refunds (in progress)

## NOWPayments final provider decision — production only, no bypass

On 12 September the owner supplied NOWPayments support's notice that sandbox is unavailable. Earlier sandbox qualification blockers are superseded, not counted as passes. The private mode setting was removed without printing or changing credentials; the file remains mode 0600. Auth, merchant coins and estimate read-only requests returned HTTP 200 with expected fields. No invoice, transfer, or public tunnel was created.

The owner withdrew the proposed runtime bypass before delivery. The final runtime selects the fixed production API and rejects stale sandbox configuration. `PAYMENTS_ENABLED=false` pauses new checkout; orders stay unpaid. Existing payment reconciliation continues, and outgoing transfers have separate explicit activation. No customer action or staff paid toggle can fabricate financial evidence. Automated tests use isolated provider fixtures; controlled low-value live transactions remain external qualification.

### R06 implementation and focused qualification

Explicit SQL migration 22 adds private cases, immutable policy/event history, exact refund obligations, separate outgoing attempts and immutable authenticated observations. Customer cases hold work, booking and credential access. Staff review, bounded appeals and customer acceptance precede resolution; cancellation releases capacity and removes stored access secrets. Refunds require customer-confirmed routing/terms, recent owner factors, cumulative receipt-backed caps and independently matched outgoing evidence. Ambiguous creation cannot repeat its financial POST. Failed retries require fresh evidence and a new approval; a later confirmation on an earlier attempt stops further issuance/authorization. Provider authenticator codes are ephemeral and do not constitute settlement evidence.

The final no-bypass domain suite, including refund authority, exact/concurrent caps, ambiguous recovery, unavailable/mismatched evidence, appeal holds, unissued cancellation and retry-after-failure fencing, passed in 25.863 seconds (`.cache/r06-final-domain2.log`). Broad golangci-lint reported zero issues and standalone gosec passed (`.cache/r06-lint5.log`, `.cache/r06-gosec5.log`). Earlier focused pause regression and full payment suites also passed; the final full gate reruns every test on staged source. New HTTP tests additionally check case/refund contract projections, private caching, CSRF, strict recipient bodies and customer/staff boundaries. Their initial setup failed at repeated fixture sign-in; it now creates a fresh customer session before commerce jobs, matching the existing HTTP-fixture pattern. HTTP and paused-checkout regressions then passed in 10.925 seconds (`.cache/r06-http3.log`); no authentication check was relaxed.

The complete browser runner passed 22 desktop and 22 mobile journeys (`.cache/r06-browser4.log`), including real application HTTP/SQL/queue paths through policy publication, checkout, forged/replayed callbacks, verified fixture settlement, cancellation case, customer-confirmed refund, owner approval, independent fixture evidence and case closure. Browser qualification exposed stale case/refund projections after another actor changed a revision. Parent order/case revision changes now reload those panels, and inputs are disabled while fresh revisions load. No assertions, test count or required checks were removed. The frontend previously passed 110 unit/component tests with full route-wrapper coverage; this does not imply full application branch coverage.

The complete mandatory root/backend/frontend/production hook is recorded in `.cache/r06-commit.log`. Stage delivery requires that gate and verified direct-main push. Funded production payments, a signed public callback, actual provider refunds and destination receipts remain unqualified; read-only connectivity and fixtures do not establish live money movement. R07–R09 remain authorized; R10/R11 remain deferred for owner review.

## R07 — Account resale and seller payouts (in progress)

The owner selected both consignment and buy-first arrangements, chosen per offer. Implementing explicit seller acceptance, unique inventory/reservations, direct buyer checkout, protected handoff and separate seller payout obligations. Commercial values and terms remain operator-entered. Draft schema work is not qualified implementation or public-launch approval.

R07 now includes explicit SQL migration 23 and Ent query mappings for immutable seller offers/events, unique account inventory and releases, reservations and protected delivery. Both per-offer arrangements are implemented; exact crypto obligations remain separate from receipt-backed refunds. Staff publication checks confirmed seller payment for buy-first arrangements. Consignment authorization checks verified buyer settlement, acknowledgement, the actual delivery dispute deadline, accepted payout delay and unresolved cases/refunds. Source eligibility is rechecked before approval, provider issue and verification.

The storefront exposes `/accounts` and `/accounts/:id`; staff edit inventory at `/admin/accounts`. Submission pages include owner offer proposals, seller acceptance/decline, protected source credentials, control verification, decision history and direct payout records. Account orders use a separate buyer bundle and explicit delivery/acknowledgement. Case rework deletes old access and resets handoff; owner takeover of pending handoff is audited. Publication accepts only reviewed, bound, sanitized screenshots from the matching seller submission. Ordinary public product projections exclude seller, submission, offer and private media identifiers.

Reservation expiry now records whether an invoice POST was claimed. Historical rows default conservatively to claimed. Never-issued requests can release inventory after retirement; possibly issued requests require fresh matching terminal zero-fund evidence, no in-flight read and no receipts. Unknown/ambiguous invoices remain held. Late funds cannot fulfill retired agreements or another current reservation. Checkout pause continues to keep orders unpaid, and maintenance continues while provider checkout is unavailable.

## R09 — Admin operations, content and privacy

The owner has authorized R09 and requires an owner-entered retention policy before deletion is enabled. R09 adds explicit SQL migration 25, immutable content/legal/retention publications, owner-reviewed privacy requests, recent-factor self exports, reviewed erasure, customer/media/consent directories, staff session revocation, intersected order/support filters, exact-unit financial reports and additional operational queues. Homepage and activity/availability email copy are editable plain text; private drafts do not affect delivery. Mandatory email routing, consent, unsubscribe and verification instructions remain enforced.

Deletion remains disabled without an explicit enabled owner publication. Original/current minimum periods are combined conservatively; manual holds require an explicit release and new review. Live business records, including seller-linked buyer orders/cases, block erasure. Erasure and new orders share the authorization fence. Database redaction is atomic; files remain pending until real worker purge confirmation after the in-flight-upload safety window. Proof and case-cited messages/images, financial contracts/routes/evidence, audits and anonymous reviews remain preserved. Ordinary customer messages and eligible private files are removed. Backups and preserved evidence are not falsely reported as physically destroyed; the owner must honor the published backup policy through deferred production operations.

The gate failed closed. Recovered 10.22 GiB by removing only regenerable Go compiler cache entries older than one day; host free space returned to roughly 11 GiB. Source files, credentials, development data and unrelated containers were not removed.

Docker also runs an unrelated `kind-control-plane`; request owner approval before a host-wide restart that interrupts it. Keep the staged implementation unchanged until recovery, rerun the full gate, then commit/push directly to main and verify the remote. Do not skip checks or treat the earlier passing browser/TLS results as a completed current gate. UX02–UX10 remain unstarted; R10/R11 remain held.

The earlier blocked status above is historical. ## UX02 — Account overview, navigation and passwordless settings (13 September 2026)

The owner requested continuation. Added a current-work account overview, desktop sidebar and mobile Overview/Orders/Messages/More navigation, plus separate profile/security/preferences routes and the existing privacy destination. Removed the crowded inline account navigation and grouped uncommon security controls in details. Staff entry is compact and retains mandatory first-factor/authenticator checks. Preserved green surfaces, accepted font families, homepage artwork and motion. UX03 chat is the next stage; R10/R11 remain held.

The new ownership-scoped `/api/v1/me/overview` API selects attention and active orders, unread public conversations and future proposed/confirmed sessions independently, rather than sampling a recent history page. Each category is bounded to five with an explicit additional-results flag. Customer unread counts use public read positions and exclude staff notes; the response contains no message body or credentials. Authorization is rechecked before and after collection. Ent remains query-only; no migration or live provider change is needed. Backend/frontend contracts and generated types agree.

Email entry now has a focused code step, numeric email-code input, focus/error feedback, pending-submit protection and explicit expiry/resend guidance under existing backend limits. Profile/preferences have separate saved confirmations. Validated local navigation returns support Google sign-in and manual re-verification; optional per-tab storage expires after ten minutes and never stores credentials or mutation data. Sensitive actions are not replayed. Account-keyed components prevent delayed data/settings from appearing after an account switch. New email subjects/sender display names, authenticator issuer, support/notification defaults and invoice descriptions use PLAYORA while preserving existing credentials, configured addresses, accepted records and internal identifiers.

Focused isolated TiDB coverage passed (5.467 seconds), exercising ownership, bounded categories, public/staff read markers, read/completion transitions, session states, schema validation, anonymous denial and stale suspended principals. Go package tests, broad golangci-lint (zero issues) and standalone gosec passed. Frontend strict types/lint, 138 component/unit tests across 49 files, enforced route coverage and both production builds passed. These are targeted results, not a substitute for the full stage gate.

First browser verification exposed saved-name whitespace and premature sign-out observation; corrected the canonical saved value and awaited the real logout response. The second run passed all desktop journeys and all other mobile journeys, but its new account test chose desktop sign-out before mobile navigation had loaded after a full page navigation. Trace inspection confirmed no logout request was sent. The helper now waits for visible account navigation before selecting the relevant control. Failed attempts are preserved as `.cache/ux02-browser-attempt1.log` and `.cache/ux02-browser-attempt2.log`; no test threshold or authorization check was weakened. Added explicit overview denial before and success after MFA for every staff role.

Reviewed rendered desktop overview and desktop/mobile profile screenshots with long names/emails. Text wraps inside its surfaces, settings have clear separate tabs, and viewport checks cover 320/390/768/1440px with modal focus/axe checks. Evidence and detailed behavior are in `docs/ux/UX02_ACCOUNT.md`. Final delivery requires the complete tracked root/backend/frontend/production hook, including fresh browser runs, retained in `.cache/ux02-commit.log`; commit/push only after that gate passes. No real payment, external Google consent setup, physical mobile-keyboard qualification or production launch is claimed.

During final return-flow review, found that an admin destination could use the storefront client router despite the independent admin route graph. Deliberately interrupted the first full gate during database integration (exit 130; `.cache/ux02-commit-attempt1.log`) before any commit. Admin returns now use document navigation; customer returns retain normal client navigation. Added a component regression and real authenticated administrator/owner browser return checks. The fresh full hook must include this correction; the interrupted gate is not a passing result.

## HF03 correction — Perceptible soft motion before HF04

The owner rejected the initial motion as barely recognizable. Reopened HF03 and held HF04 onward for review of this correction. Revisited the rendered boosting.pro homepage/video. The accepted master/JPEG are unchanged; the lazy scene remains 201,443 bytes within the existing 210,000-byte limit. No new dependency, backend, production or live-provider change.

Added a browser regression against the actual rendered pixels: more than 6 CSS px between head centers in opposite poses, less than 0.75px foot-center movement. This catches the previous head-static effect even when its animation clock advances. WebKit desktop/mobile passed and measured 13.15px / 6.77px head-center movement with zero foot movement. Its recorded local RAF p95 was 44ms / 28ms; these are scheduling samples under concurrent recording, not physical-device FPS guarantees. Type and lint checks passed. Focused final Firefox evidence and the complete tracked frontend/root gate are recorded in `.cache/hf03-revision-pixels-firefox.log` and `.cache/hf03-correction-commit.log`; delivery requires their successful completion. Continuing Firefox qualification in an isolated Linux image from the existing pinned frontend build, with no runtime environment files or backend access. The Mac startup limitation remains explicit; no system privacy settings were changed.

## 21 October 2026 — FORMS172 minimal customer forms

The owner asked for every user-facing form to be minimal, aligned and easy, with each change decided by a senior UI/UX designer, plus removal of the homepage “Availability is shown on each game page.” note, then deployment. An independent designer audited all customer form families (live read-only, fixtures and source) and decided shared-system and per-form changes; four rendered review rounds ended in PASS ([record](redesign/reviews/FORMS172_DESIGN.md)). Local focused checks passed: 991 component tests, 146 fixture browser cases with new alignment regressions, types/lint/format, repository checks and three production builds. Legal, payment-safety, credential, fee/refund and authored catalog text are preserved; F-35 catalog help suggestions remain an owner decision. Signed `6eaad20` is now deployed and verified at schema47 after the owner's exact approval, including required Go 1.27.3 and Vitest 5.0.3 security remediations, exact CI39680232797, image release39682617820, server preflight, eight-service verification and 12 live read-only Chromium cases.
